Prime law\n"},{"number":1102,"text":"\n"},{"number":1103,"text":"Using the logically complete candidate Ontology, derive the Prime semantic\n"},{"number":1104,"text":"atoms before proposing top-level realisation units. Whole-family Prime is\n"},{"number":1105,"text":"part of the evidence used to accept the Ontology basis. Target design,\n"},{"number":1106,"text":"IACS, modules, source functions, schemas and tests project those accepted\n"},{"number":1107,"text":"atoms; they do not originate Prime meaning. Prime applies both to each\n"},{"number":1108,"text":"semantic atom and to the candidate family as a whole (P3, P2;\n"},{"number":1109,"text":"rc.2 #5-prime-law).\n"},{"number":1110,"text":"\n"},{"number":1111,"text":"Prime-conformance applies to these realisation projections: functions,\n"},{"number":1112,"text":"classes, carrier types, top-level schema records and modules. Typed\n"},{"number":1113,"text":"closure by itself is not sufficient reason to create a new top-level type.\n"},{"number":1114,"text":"The hard vocabulary of this law (P1):\n"},{"number":1115,"text":"\n"},{"number":1116,"text":"- Irreducible Architectural Carrier Set (IACS): the smallest lawful\n"},{"number":1117,"text":"  top-level carrier family required for one boundary;\n"},{"number":1118,"text":"- Subordinate Payload: payload detail that exists inside that boundary but\n"},{"number":1119,"text":"  is not top-level by default;\n"},{"number":1120,"text":"- Promotion Test: the explicit test a Subordinate Payload passes before it\n"},{"number":1121,"text":"  becomes a top-level type; and\n"},{"number":1122,"text":"- Boundary Inflation: the defect where subordinate payload detail is\n"},{"number":1123,"text":"  promoted into extra peer types without irreducible need.\n"},{"number":1124,"text":"\n"},{"number":1125,"text":"A top-level realisation unit is Prime-conformant only when it projects one\n"},{"number":1126,"text":"or more accepted Prime atoms without introducing, duplicating or\n"},{"number":1127,"text":"reinterpreting semantic authority. A topological or effect boundary is\n"},{"number":1128,"text":"first derived and accepted as a Prime atom. Source-code size and\n"},{"number":1129,"text":"one-to-one correspondence with an atom are immaterial (P3, P6).\n"},{"number":1130,"text":"\n"},{"number":1131,"text":"Typical lawful reasons to introduce a new top-level realisation unit are a\n"},{"number":1132,"text":"new feature topology or branch in the admitted work graph, a new typed\n"},{"number":1133,"text":"carrier transformation, a new effect boundary, a new validation or\n"},{"number":1134,"text":"admission boundary, and a new projection boundary. A realisation unit is\n"},{"number":1135,"text":"not Prime-conformant when it exists only to: shorten a call site without\n"},{"number":1136,"text":"creating a new semantic boundary; aggregate unrelated behaviour for\n"},{"number":1137,"text":"convenience; preserve an old interface behind a new name; shuttle data\n"},{"number":1138,"text":"through one more wrapper layer; hide mutation, fallback or orchestration\n"},{"number":1139,"text":"that should remain explicit; name one more payload variation that carries\n"},{"number":1140,"text":"no independent authority; turn internal record detail into public schema\n"},{"number":1141,"text":"surface without an irreducible boundary; mirror another top-level carrier\n"},{"number":1142,"text":"with only small field variation; or respond to typing discomfort by\n"},{"number":1143,"text":"multiplying peer classes instead of closing one governing carrier family\n"},{"number":1144,"text":"(P3, P2).\n"},{"number":1145,"text":"\n"},{"number":1146,"text":"This is Occam's razor for design modules: prefer composition over\n"},{"number":1147,"text":"convenience extraction; prefer existing lawful functions over new\n"},{"number":1148,"text":"wrappers; prefer fewer, sharper boundaries over many vague helper\n"},{"number":1149,"text":"functions; prefer fewer, sharper carrier families over many payload-shaped\n"},{"number":1150,"text":"peer types. The purpose of the Prime law is not to minimise line count; it\n"},{"number":1151,"text":"is to stop semantic fragmentation and helper sprawl from becoming\n"},{"number":1152,"text":"architecture (P2).\n"},{"number":1153,"text":"\n"}]},{"end_line":1168,"heading":"DESIGN_MODULE/6. The general\n"},{"number":1363,"text":"reconstruction rule is [ACTIVATION §1 Intake](ACTIVATION.md#intake); this\n"},{"number":1364,"text":"clause states what design evidence supplies to it.\n"},{"number":1365,"text":"\n"},{"number":1366,"text":"Given an accepted material-frame basis, as determined by the material\n"},{"number":1367,"text":"semantic boundary definition (§5), and a proposed bounded action, the\n"},{"number":1368,"text":"selected method basis together with the accepted design is sufficient to\n"},{"number":1369,"text":"recover every governing relation in the action's causally closed affected\n"},{"number":1370,"text":"set, and to decide whether the action preserves, changes, duplicates or\n"},{"number":1371,"text":"violates each of those relations. The two inputs are distinct and neither\n"},{"number":1372,"text":"substitutes for the other: the selected method describes which relations\n"},{"number":1373,"text":"are required and how to reason about them; the accepted design supplies\n"},{"number":1374,"text":"the concrete invariant values. A design that restates method decision law\n"},{"number":1375,"text":"to satisfy this rule reproduces the local-method duplication this law\n"},{"number":1376,"text":"exists to remove (P1, P6).\n"},{"number":1377,"text":"\n"},{"number":1378,"text":"| Decision | The action, against a governing relation | Core value |\n"},{"number":1379,"text":"|---|---|---|\n"},{"number":1380,"text":"| preserves | leaves the relation intact and operates inside it | `satisfied` |\n"},{"number":1381,"text":"| changes | alters the relation itself, and therefore re-enters at that relation before acceptance | `invalid_basis` |\n"},{"number":1382,"text":"| duplicates | establishes an equivalent path that is explicitly disposed as a constituent, replica, replacement or deliberately independent realisation | `satisfied` |\n"},{"number":1383,"text":"| violates | contradicts the relation, or establishes an equivalent path that is left undisposed | `falsified` |\n"},{"number":1384,"text":"\n"},{"number":1385,"text":"The boundary between changes and violates is the locus of acceptance, not\n"},{"number":1386,"text":"the size of the delta: an otherwise lawful change accepted inside the\n"},{"number":1387,"text":"acting frame rather than at the relation it changes is a violation. The\n"},{"number":1388,"text":"boundary between duplicates and violates is disposition, not equivalence:\n"},{"number":1389,"text":"an equivalent path is lawful when disposed and a defect when not (P4b,\n"},{"number":1390,"text":"P6). Sufficiency is bound to a declared action and its affected set; it is\n"},{"number":1391,"text":"not a claim over an unbounded space of possible future changes, and the\n"},{"number":1392,"text":"performance of any particular reviewer is not evidence either way (P0).\n"},{"number":1393,"text":"\n"},{"number":1394,"text":"The four decisions are a refinement of the core result algebra\n"},{"number":1395,"text":"([FOUNDATIONS §4 Results](FOUNDATIONS.md#results)). For `changes`, the\n"},{"number":1401,"text":"relation itself must change at its own locus before the action can be\n"},{"number":1402,"text":"accepted, so the current governing basis cannot support accepting it in\n"},{"number":1403,"text":"the acting frame, as for the disposition `re_enter` of\n"},{"number":1404,"text":"[ACTIVATION §3](ACTIVATION.md#disposition). For `violates`, the\n"},{"number":1405,"text":"contradiction or the undisposed equivalent path is the counterexample. An\n"},{"number":1406,"text":"evaluation that reaches none of the four decisions returns the core value\n"},{"number":1407,"text":"its cause selects, `indeterminate` where the admissible evidence cannot\n"},{"number":1408,"text":"decide, naming what would decide (FOUNDATIONS §4 Results); where the\n"},{"number":1409,"text":"cause is that the selected method basis and the accepted design do not\n"},{"number":1410,"text":"suffice, the case falsifies this law, as stated below. The mapping is\n"},{"number":1411,"text":"new in this corpus. Packaging, syntax,\n"},{"number":17,"text":"compilation, presence, invocation or local behaviour never substitutes for a\n"},{"number":18,"text":"semantic, authority-bearing, installed, release or end-to-end claim because\n"},{"number":19,"text":"the weaker property is easier to measure (P6; conserves\n"},{"number":20,"text":"[UP-001](stdo://releases/v2.5.1-rc.2/standards/SPEC_METHOD.md#proof-target-identity-and-adequacy-stdo-up-001)).\n"},{"number":21,"text":"\n"},{"number":22,"text":"Where a claim about a construction concerns its recursive or repeated\n"},{"number":23,"text":"work, it is one of the four kinds the construction relation keeps apart,\n"},{"number":24,"text":"preservation, progress, termination and outcome satisfaction\n"},{"number":25,"text":"([ACTIVATION §1](ACTIVATION.md#construction-relation)), and states which.\n"},{"number":26,"text":"Its witness is an assertion whose predicate is the full obligation, a\n"},{"number":27,"text":"necessary condition of it or part of a sufficient conjunction, and the\n"},{"number":28,"text":"claim states which. Its expected result has assertion provenance and its\n"},{"number":29,"text":"execution has execution provenance, as\n"},{"number":30,"text":"[FOUNDATIONS §4 Assertions and observation](FOUNDATIONS.md#assertions-and-observation)\n"},{"number":31,"text":"and [Parallel derivation and mutual assurance](FOUNDATIONS.md#parallel-derivation-and-mutual-assurance)\n"},{"number":32,"text":"define them; a passing check of a necessary condition establishes that\n"},{"number":33,"text":"condition and no more (P1, P6).\n"},{"number":34,"text":"\n"},{"number":35,"text":"Boundary congruence.
